### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 2 日

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露：  
就溫室氣體管理策略而言，宏捷科的方向主要有六， (1) 配合政府環保政策
執行溫室氣體盤查， (2) 將空壓機安裝無耗氣排水器，減少運作電能消耗，
(3) 廠房照明設計以自然照明替代電能，並將高耗電之傳統燈具改為 LED 之
省電燈具 (持續改善中 )， (4) 廠內設備逐步汰換 IE3 等級以上之高效能馬達，
(5) 空壓機更換效能較佳的機台及降載， (6) 無塵室氣流循環驅動及空氣過濾
設備降載節能。  
再就溫室氣體排放減量之預算與計畫而言，宏捷科落實的途徑主要有五， (1)
生產程序優化， (2) 廠內節能措施進行， (3) 電力照明之替代及管理， (4) 馬達
汰舊換新 (IE3 等級以上 )， (5) 增設含氟排氣處理設備。  
至於宏捷科溫室氣體排放量減量目標則為針對電能減量以降低間接排放之
溫室氣體數量、減少 PFCs 氣體排放以減少直接排放之溫室氣體數量。  
手機相關產品出貨持續暢旺， 2Q26 稅後淨利 2.78 億元， QoQ-6.23% ：  
宏捷科為砷化鎵晶圓代工廠，目前總產能為 2 萬片 /月 (以 6 吋晶圓為計算基
礎 )，僅次於同業穩懋 (3105 TT) 的第二大廠，但就現有無塵室空間而言，最
大產能規模可達 4 萬片 /月，換言之，在市場需求增溫時，宏捷科僅需購置、
安裝機台，並通過客戶認證 (部份客戶甚至未要求認證 )後，即可擴大產能規
模。  
宏捷科的產品線包括 PA(power amplifier ，功率放大器 )、 switch/LNA( 開關
/low noise amplifier 低雜訊放大器 )等射頻 (RF，radio frequency) 產品，其中，
PA 多以 HBT 製程生產， switch/LNA 則多以 HEMT 製程生產，此外，宏捷
科亦生產光電 (optical) 產品線，主要為供 3D 感測光源之用的 VCSEL 。若再
就各產品線的終端應用而言，訂單則多來自 Wi-Fi 、手機 (phone) ，近幾年營
收比重分別為 30~60% 、 35~75% 。  
中美晶 (5483 TT) 於 08/2020 參與宏捷科的私募，宏捷科除與中美晶共同進
軍 GaN( 氮化鎵 )領域外，並可藉所取得的資金投入研發，至於中美晶則得以
在宏捷科量產的過程中供應自身生產的 GaN on SiC( 碳化矽基氮化鎵 )材料，
亦即雙方合作將發揮垂直整合綜效。  
2Q26 宏捷科營收 12.98 億元， QoQ+6.23% ，原因在於手機相關產品營收增
加至 9.49 億元， QoQ+7.55% ，不過，受到磊晶片 (epi wafer) 、黃金等原材料
漲價影響，毛利率降低至 29.98%(1Q26 為 34.63%) ，營業利益 QoQ-13.40% ，
稅後淨利 2.78 億元， QoQ-6.23% 。  
1H26 宏捷科營收 25.21 億元，為歷史次高， YoY+44.36% ，其中，手機相關
產品營收 18.32 億元， YoY+56.16% ，原因有二， (1) 中國手機 PA 廠打入韓
國中階手機供應鏈，且自 2026 年開始大量出貨 (過去中、高階手機用 PA 多
由美國業者供應 )， (2) 中國手機 PA 廠供中國手機品牌業者高階機種使用的
產品多仍交由宏捷科代工 (搭載於中、低階手機的 PA 則已積極轉單至中國
同業 )。  
2H26 毛利率仍受原物料成本偏高影響，但 1H27 獲利展望有機會轉佳：  
展望 2H26 、 2027 年，預估 2H26 營收與 1H26 相去有限，但 2027 年則有成

### Page 3

個股報告

3 群益投顧 
長空間。若就產品結構而言，首先，雖然手機相關產品雖然必須面對 2026
年整體手機市場需求因記憶體等零組件漲價、缺料衝擊而降溫的負面大環
境，但持續受惠於中國手機 PA 廠打入韓國中階手機供應鏈、中國品牌手機
高階機種所需 PA 代工地位穩固，以及美國大客戶 (IC 設計公司 )對中國、韓
國旗艦機滲透率開始提高的衍生商機發揮。  
其次， Wi-Fi 相關產品 2H26 、 2027 年的動能則有三， (1)router Wi-Fi PA 訂
單數量隨 Wi-Fi 7 市佔率提高而增加 ，(2) 無人機 PA 1H26 營收 YoY+194.6% ，
由於客戶家數由 1~2 家增加至 4~5 家，後續營收成長性仍高， (3) 受惠於美
國 IDM 廠逐步退出中國市場，美國大客戶、中國手機 PA 廠的手機 Wi-Fi 
PA 陸續打入中國手機品牌廠的供應鏈，且多數訂單來自旗艦機、高階機種。
最後，在新產品的部份，包括 datacom 用 VCSEL( 後續亦將開發 PD 及更高
波長的產品 )、濾波器、消費型 LiDAR( 終端應用如車用、掃地機器人、割草
機等 )均已量產， solar cell 則將於 2026 年年底量產，惟對宏捷科的營收貢獻
程度則均尚待觀察。  
不過，由於中、低階手機舊機種相關產品報價不易調整，旗艦機、高階手機
舊機種的漲價幅度亦難以全數反應原材料成本漲幅，預估 2H26 宏捷科的毛
利率仍受到原材料採購價格偏高影響，惟待新機種相關產品陸續上市後，預
估 1H27 毛利率有機會回升，獲利展望亦得以轉佳。  
雖 2H26 獲利展望仍保守，但 1H27 有機會轉佳，群益對宏捷科的投資建議
為 Trading Buy ：  
由於磊晶片、黃金等原材料成本偏高，宏捷科 2H26 獲利表現不易較 2Q26
明顯改善，不過，待新機種相關產品陸續上市後，預估 1H27 毛利率有機會
回升，獲利展望亦得以轉佳，故群益對宏捷科的投資建議為 Trading Buy 。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

福邦投顧 研究部
2026/09

### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
結論
⚫ AI算力需求推升高速光互聯市場。美國四大CSP持續擴大AI資料中心建置，為帶動高速光
模組需求成長的主要動能。現階段高速光模組出貨仍以800G為主，1.6T預計自2027年逐步
放量，也帶動矽光(SiPh)模組滲透率提高。
⚫ 矽光模組非新興產品，外型仍沿用既有可插拔光模組規格，但相較傳統光模組，SiPh具備
元件高度整合及晶圓級量產優勢，隨高速傳輸所需而獲廣泛採用。然而，雷射晶片受限於
上游InP基板缺貨而供應吃緊。為確保貨源，部分業者已透過長約或預付款方式鎖定供應，
使掌握貨源者在市場上更具競爭優勢。
⚫ CPO現階段仍面臨光耦合、測試與量產等製程挑戰；對CSP而言，CPO改變其既有可插拔光
模組的維修與更換模式，加上NPO、XPO等替代架構持續發展，使CPO雖已進入量產階段
，短期滲透率仍偏低。展望未來，傳輸頻寬持續提升，為降低傳輸損耗與功率，光引擎將
逐步靠近交換器ASIC或加速卡，CPO仍具導入需求。此外，NVIDIA持續擴大NVLink

Fusion生態系，擴大CPO潛在應用市場，惟規模部署時程仍待帶動頻寬需求的新應用興起。
⚫ 相關個股：聯亞(3081)、IET-KY(4971)、寰宇-KY(4991)、聯鈞(3450)、訊芯-KY(6451)、華
星光(4979)、光聖(6442)、波若威(3163)、上詮(3363)、大立光(3008)

### Page 4

AI算力需求推升高速光互聯市場

### Page 5

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI DC建置，光通訊需求持續成長
• 生成式AI浪潮帶動美國CSP進入DC建置競賽，2023年仍為投資初期，2024年CapEx明顯加速
，預期2026年美國四大CSP—Amazon、Google、微軟、Meta ，CapEx合計增至7,500億美元，
帶動高速光互聯(networking)需求。
• Amazon更指出，預期AWS算力供不應求情況將延續至2027年，且2028年需求能見度已高。
• 2022年底ChatGPT推出帶動生成式AI浪潮，惟當時COVID-19疫情剛結束，Amazon和Meta因
疫情期間大幅擴張基礎建設而下調2023年CapEx，使四大CSP整體CapEx較2022年略低。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

### Page 6

6【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
預估2031年光模組市場營收將達1,120億美元
資料來源：Yole, 福邦投顧彙整
• 市調機構預估，至2031年光模組市場營收將達1,120億美元，CAGR成長30%。AI DC為營收
貢獻主力， 佔比達9成。
• 2025年合計資料中心與電信商的光模組營收為230億美元，當時兩者營收分別為180億與50億
美元，差異有限；2031年資料中心的光模組營收達1,010億，電信商僅增為110億美元。
圖2、2025與2031年光模組市場規模比較

### Page 7

7【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光互連應用由機櫃延伸至跨資料中心
資料來源：Ciena, 福邦投顧彙整
圖3、資料中心Scale Up、Scale Out與Scale Across架構
• 目前光通訊產品出貨集中於Scale Up與Scale Out應用；隨資料中心建置規模擴大及耗電量增加
，受限於電力與土地供給，資料中心朝分散式建置發展，並透過光纖串聯多座資料中心以進行
共同運算，帶動Scale Across需求。
• Scale Up為機櫃內元件與設備互連；Scale Out為資料中心內不同機櫃間互連；Scale Across為
資料中心互連。

### Page 8

8【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
依傳輸距離採用不同光互聯技術
資料來源：Ciena, 福邦投顧彙整
• Scale Up傳輸距離較短，以成本較低的主動銅纜(ACC)為主；隨傳輸速率增加，銅線難以維
持訊號品質，便會採用光纖兩端配置光收發模組的主動光纜(AOC)。
• Scale Out主流採用IMDD可插拔(pluggable)光模組。
• NPO和CPO為應用於Scale Out和Scale up的新技術，藉縮短光引擎與交換器ASIC距離以降低
高速傳輸的電訊號損耗。NPO的光引擎鄰近交換器ASIC擺放；CPO的光引擎與交換器ASIC
共同封裝。
• Scale Across傳輸距離約20公里以上，需採用相干光(coherent)技術來維持訊號品質，供應商
相對有限，以歐美系業者為主。
圖4、三類架構的光互聯技術需求
Scale UpScale OutScale Across

矽光子技術與供應鏈發展

### Page 10

10【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI資料中心的光互聯台廠供應鏈
• AI DC 高速互連升級，帶動 AOC/ACC 與800G/1.6T可插拔光模組需求成長，並推升矽光
(SiPh)模組滲透率。台廠AOC供應商包括佳必琪與源傑。
• 台廠受惠範圍由光模組元件與封裝，進一步延伸至CW Laser、光學次模組封裝及 PIC 代工
• 高密度光纖佈線需求同步增加，帶動 MPO/Jumper、Fiber Harness、Shuffle Module 等產品需
求，台廠供應商包括光聖、波若威、佳必琪、貿聯-KY。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光模組朝高速與高密度整合演進
• AI DC帶動高速光模組需求，2026年以400G、800G為主，1.6T開始放量；2027年1.6T出貨量
將明顯提升，並預期2028年導入3.2T。市調機構LightCounting預估，隨800G、1.6T等高速光
模組需求增加，矽光模組滲透率將提升，2026年矽光模組銷售額YoY成長110%，占整體光模
組市場約50%。
• 光模組速率提升是透過增加光通道或提高單通道速率來實現，以每通道200G為例，800G需4
個光通道，1.6T則增至8個，通道數增加所需的調變器、PD等元件數量也隨之增加，使耦合
及封裝複雜度提高，凸顯矽光模組的整合優勢
資料來源：LightCounting，千才，福邦投顧彙整

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
矽光PIC高度整合光元件並以半導體製程量產
• 矽光模組是指搭載矽光PIC的光模組，矽光PIC透過半導體製程，將調變器、波導等光學功能
整合於晶片，減少離散元件組裝及光學對準步驟，並有利於晶圓級量產。
• 2016年英特爾即推出矽光子光模組，惟早期高速傳輸需求有限，矽光PIC整合效益尚不顯著；
隨800G、1.6T以上高速光模組放量，通道數及光元件數量增加，矽光PIC整合優勢浮現。
• 現階段矽光模組仍沿用沿用OSFP、QSFP-DD既有可插拔光模組外型。
• 過去光通訊PIC多採III-V族材料，雷射與光元件可單晶整合，但市場規模較小且製程專用，多
以IDM方式生產；矽光PIC採CMOS相容製程，可導入成熟矽晶圓代工模式，實現規模量產與
降低成本，然無法與雷射整合。
資料來源：博通，福邦投顧彙整
圖7、傳統光模組與矽光子光模組架構比較

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
矽光PIC需求擴大，專業分工成形
• 矽光PIC晶圓代工目前以Tower半導體量產規模及客戶能見度最高；聯電亦積極切入，2025年
取得比利時半導體研究機構imec的12吋矽光子製程技術授權，2026年於新加坡12吋廠量產
1.6T PIC，預計2027年向更多客戶開放矽光代工平台。
• 除英特爾外，其餘具備矽光PIC設計能力業者並未單獨銷售PIC，而是將PIC與EIC、DSP整合
為光引擎(optical engine)後出售。
• 2023年英特爾出售光模組事業予Jabil，專注於提供整合雷射的矽光PIC。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

InP基板業者宣布擴產 供給能見度仍有限
• 三家InP基板業者皆宣布擴產，產能均呈倍數增長，惟未揭露既有產能，尚難推估未來InP基
板實際供給量。
• AXT分別獲Coherent及Lumentum預付約2,200萬及8,700萬美元，用於6吋InP基板擴產及產能
預留。

• Lumentum、Cohernet、博通採IDM模式，具備從LD設計、磊晶、晶圓製程至雷射封裝能力，
並視產能與產品需求委外，如Coherent將部分LD封裝委由聯鈞生產。

光學元件整合成TOSA；高功率CW雷射可用於矽光模組，或CPO/NPO的外部雷射光源。

### Page 18

CPO發展趨勢與導入挑戰

### Page 19

19【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO於3.2T世代有望加速滲透
• 台積電表示CPO模組將於2026年下半年量產，惟依供應鏈資訊，初期出貨量仍低、市場規模
有限。市調機構預估，CPO光模組出貨量將自2027年起明顯成長，2030年達約6,000萬顆；
滲透率部分，將由2027年的11%提升至2028年的19%，2030年進一步達38%。
• 業界普遍認為，傳輸速率進入3.2T世代後，CPO在功耗與頻寬密度上的優勢將更為明顯。博
通也表示，CPO的光互聯耗電量較可插拔光模組減少70%。
資料來源：Lightcounting，Dell’Oro, 福邦投顧彙整
圖10、CPO出貨量與滲透率預估

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO推動光電共封裝，FAU成光耦合關鍵元件
• CPO為光電共封裝架構，將 PIC與EIC 整合為光引擎(OE)，再與Switch ASIC共封裝於同一載
板，目標降低電訊號傳輸距離與功耗。
• 雷射仍放置於交換器機箱前面板，且維持可插拔，以方便更換。
• 目前台積電以COUPE平台整合PIC與EIC成光引擎，然提高光耦合效率、降低光進入 PIC 的
損耗，成為CPO主要瓶頸之一，也使 FAU 成為關鍵元件。
資料來源：Photonics Spectra, 福邦投顧彙整

### Page 21

21【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
FAU難以晶片化，攸關CPO光耦合效率與量產時程
• 目前台積電COUPE平台優先採用光柵(grating)耦合架構，FAU以黏膠固定於光學引擎上
方。光線平行射出，需以反射鏡導入PIC；COUPE端利用矽透鏡將光聚焦至光柵耦合器
，也配置金屬反射鏡回收散射光，提升光耦合效率。
• 光耦合效率攸關光學元件設計與光纖精密對位。FAU除涉及光學設計外，光纖亦需精準
置入V-groove，並完成固定、端面研磨與對位封裝，工序繁雜而被視為CPO規模量產瓶
頸之一。
• FAU投入業者主要包括線材廠、傳統FAU業者及光學元件廠。例如，光聖旗下合聖推出
以Metalens搭配反射鏡的可拆卸式FAU；大立光則推出配置80顆微型鏡頭、整合反射鏡
與稜鏡功能的PMLA，並進一步布局多層堆疊光纖。線材廠方面，以波若威、佳必琪與
貿聯為代表；傳統FAU業者則以上銓為代表。
資料來源：TSMC, 福邦投顧彙整
圖12、TSMC COUPE的FAU光耦合架構
大立光、合聖針對反射鏡推出解決方案

### Page 22

22【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
FAU技術升級聚焦提升耦光效率與縮短對位時間
• FAU主要發展方向為可拆卸設計、多層光纖堆疊與被動對位。其中，可拆卸式方案以美、日
業者布局較積極，主要考量FAU或光纖生損壞可直接更換，避免拆除整組光學引擎，降低維
修難度及交換器損壞風險。
• 目前FAU多採主動對位，透過反覆微調位置並量測光功率，找出最佳耦合點後固定，雖可補
償製造與組裝誤差，但組裝時間長。被動對位仰賴高精度製程與定位結構，將精度要求前移
，以簡化後段組裝並提升量產效率
資料來源：各業者, 福邦投顧彙整
圖13、FAU正發展可拆卸、多層堆疊及被動對準
日商Senko的可拆卸FAU
Teramount雙層光纖設計使
光纖量從單層的32條增為
64條。
Teramount與高塔半導體合作開發Bump-ready wafer，
即在PIC晶圓階段預先製作光學耦合與對位結構。

### Page 23

23【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
NPO持續演進 成為Scale Up另一選擇
• NPO被視為CPO折衷方案，光引擎置於鄰近交換器ASIC，降低傳輸損耗且保留維修彈性。
• 華為認為NPO具備提供400G/lane的潛力，3.2T世代未必需全面導入CPO。
• 博通、Marvell、Coherent皆已展示或推出NPO方案，光源架構部分，Marvell主打無DSP、配
置CW雷射；博通展示VCSEL方案，Coherent同時推出VCSEL和CW方案，VCSEL功耗低，
晶片尺寸小，可高密度排列，並與光引擎整合封裝，然傳輸距離有限，適合Scale-up應用。
• 被視為Marvell合作業者的華星光，預期2027年下半開始量產1.6T NPO模組，2028年出貨量
體將較為顯著。
資料來源：華為, 福邦投顧彙整

### Page 24

24【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
XPO重塑可插拔模組 挑戰CPO的Scale Out市場
• 美網通業者Arista於2026年成立XPO MSA，並展示首代支援200G/lane，頻寬12.8T的可插拔
XPO模組，後續將發展400G/lane、單模組頻寬25.6T，惟量產與規模商用時程尚未明朗。
XPO MSA成員橫跨CSP、ASIC、光元件與光模組供應鏈，包括微軟、Marvell、Coherent、
Lumentum及新易盛等。Arista為Ethernet交換器設備商，主導XPO規格與生態系發展。
• XPO主打Scale-out應用，強調單一XPO模組可取代8顆OSFP，在有限機箱空間內提高整體傳
輸頻寬。由於XPO採全新模組機構，交換器須配合重新設計供電、散熱及相關介面，目前規
格制定與系統平台仍以Arista為主；儘管XPO MSA讓CSP在光模組供應商選擇上更具彈性，
但在其他交換器業者尚未跟進前，導入XPO亦可能意味對Arista交換器平台的更高依賴。
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Open CPX以標準化介面挑戰NVIDIA封閉式CPO架構
• 美系光傳輸設備商Ciena與Coherent，以及連接器業者Molex、Samtec推動Open CPX MSA，
透過標準介面讓讓光引擎可拆卸，獨立於ASIC，提高光引擎與ASIC的選擇彈性。
• Open CPX強調架構選擇彈性，CSP可依系統需求採用NPO或CPO，雷射可選外部雷射或整合
式雷射方案。
• Ciena已推出採Open CPX架構的光引擎Vesta，，並以16 顆6.4T光引擎打造總頻寬102.4T的系
統。Ciena於3Q26法說表示，Vesta樣品已售出，預計2027年開始貢獻營收、2028年放量。

Molex 和Samtec。

### Page 26

26【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
各種光互聯架構重塑供應鏈價值分配
資料來源：福邦投顧彙整
受惠供應鏈 承壓供應鏈
CPO/NPO 光引擎、外部雷射、FAU 傳統可插拔光模組
XPO 可插拔光模組、雷射 相對有限
Open CPX 可拆卸光引擎、連接器、雷射、FAU 封閉式CPO供應鏈
• CPO和NPO的供應鏈差異有限，皆由交換器ASIC或加速卡業者主導系統架構，光引擎、雷
射、FAU、連接器等供應鏈皆配合設計。若CPO滲透率提高，將壓縮傳統可插拔光模組需求
，但具雷射組裝能力的業者仍可供應外部雷射
• CPO的光耦合效率需求提高，使FAU重要性提升。
• XPO藉提高模組頻寬，延續可插拔方案在高速傳輸的生命週期。
• Open CPX採用標準介面實現光引擎可拆卸，由於PCB板端需配置CPX連接器，使連接器重
要性提升。
• 不論採用何種光互連架構，隨AI資料中心傳輸頻寬與光路數持續增加，高密度佈線需求均將
提升，帶動fiber jumper、fiber harness及fiber shuffle需求，如光聖已切入Google資料中心光纖

連接供應鏈將明顯受惠。
表5、不同光互連架構受惠與承壓供應鏈比較

### Page 27

27【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO長期具導入需求，惟時程仍具不確定性
• CPO在Scale-out面臨傳統可插拔光模組、XPO與NPO等架構競爭；相較下，台積電認為CPO
在Scale-up更能發揮高頻寬、低功耗與短距離互連優勢，朝Optical I/O發展。
• Scale-up 架構目前存在技術路線分歧—Narrow & Fast 與 Wide & Slow；前者持續提高單通道
速率，以NVIDIA為代表，後者增加道數來擴充頻寬，微軟MOSAIC為較典型方案。
• 展望未來，儘管CPO導入時程受系統架構與成本效益影響，但隨運算規模持續擴大，銅互連
瓶頸將推動光引擎進一步靠近交換器ASIC或XPU，CPO長期導入趨勢不變。 再者，NVIDIA
亦透過NVLink Fusion持續擴大Fabric生態系，使採用客製XPU的業者仍可導入NVLink架構
，擴大CPO潛在應用市場。
資料來源：TSMC, 福邦投顧彙整

Source: Bloomberg
Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

### Page 3

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 3
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and

covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

In 3Q24, ACES passed product qualification with ODMs for the high-speed

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

## Document 244: GFHK - MediaTek update

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - MediaTek update.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

100% EMIB-T, 100% Market Share in v9  
C Progress Intact, with >$170 Annualized EPS 
Potential

Maintained  Buy 
 Target price NT$ 6,650

Street-high TPU estimates and up TP to NT$6,650: MTK fell 7% yesterday on market noise 
around a potential v8t delay, insufficient EMIB supply, and share‑loss concerns in v9. We hold 
an opposite view, as our research indicates the v8t/Zebrafish remains intact, demand is well 
above the 2027 CoWoS allocation of 180k, EMIB ‑T/substrate yields and supply are tracking 
better than expected, and MTK retains 100% share in the v9/2nm. We remain constructive, 
as MTK’s datacenter business is well-positioned for share gains in TPU across volume, value 
and workloads. Beyond TPU, we expect improving visibility on the 2nd ASIC project, likely 
SpaceX ASIC (our report: Likely SpaceX Win on May 27) instead of AI6, in 4Q26. We revised 
our earnings estimates by 0%/+3% in 2026E/2027E and accordingly lift TP from NT$6,420 to 
NT$6,650. We now forecast its datacenter ASIC to be NT$2.8bn/$18.6bn/NT$75.7bn.  
Market noise around adding CoWoS on top of EMIB ‑T makes little sense : Contrary to 
recent sell‑side reports suggesting CoWoS as a way to improve v9 supply, we continue to 
believe v9, both Humufish and Triggerfish, will be 100% on EMIB‑T, with life‑cycle volume of 
>5m (vs bears at >2m). We reiterate our view ( Intel: Yield Expansion  on Sept 15) that two 
major suppliers have improved yields from 20–25% on initial testing vehicles in June to ~45% 
on the August vehicles and ~50% currently. This is on track to reach the >50% yield target in 
1Q27. Supported by three substrate suppliers with dedicated lines (incl. Gama fab), we expect 
small‑volume substrate deliveries in mid‑2027 of ~100k and mass‑volume by 4Q27.  
Market noise around MTK’s share loss in v9 makes little sense: Contrary to worries over 
MTK’s share loss in v9, we expect MTK to gain 100% share in v9, with Humufish for training 
and Triggerfish for inferencing, while believe that there is no other 2nm project during the time 
frame. Near term, the key milestone to watch is the engineering tape‑out in Nov, and as noted, 
we believe the passing bar is not high and believe the current chipset testing at TSMC for 
both compute tile and Serdes is largely on track. For v10, we still expect MTK to obtain the 
major SKU, Icefish, while AMD+MRVL or AVGO is competing with the other SKU, Merope.  
Market noise around MTK’s v8t/Zebrafish overly bearish: Contrary to worries over MTK’s 
v8t/Zebrafish delay to 2Q27, we still expect the SKU to start shipments in 4Q26, backed by 
mgmt’s guidance of >$2bn ASIC revenue in 2026. We believe the demand is much higher 
than the 2027’s CoWoS allocation of 180k and expect the life cycle to be well extended.  
Risks: (1) AI demand slowdown; (2) consumer demand; (3) peer competition.

Jeff Pu, CFA

5

Valuation and Recommendation  
Maintain Buy with TP of $6,650 
We rate MTK (2454 TT) Buy and set our TP at NT$ 6,650 which is based on 45x our 2027E EPS 
estimate or 18x 2028E EPS. We raise our 2027 multiple to reflect higher earnings growth in 2027/2028, 
but it suggests an undemanding valuation for 2028. MTK’s P/E traded at an average of 18x since 2021 
but was up to an average of 2 2x since 1Q24 when the company starts to see the AI op portunity. Our 
target multiple for 2027 is much higher than the mid-cycle level given Google TPU momentum and our 
bullish estimate of its 2027E/2028E earnings growth.  
We adopt 2027 and/or 2028 as valuation basis to capture its datacenter ASIC potential which is the key 
market focus  and expect the business to take off starting 2027.  We adopt P/E methodology as we 
believe the market now mainly focuses on the company’s earnings outlook, and we usually apply P/E 
method to downstream firms.  
Fundamentally, w e are positive on MTK, as it is well -positioned to expand its value position in 
smartphone SoCs amid the edge -AI trend, as well as the expansion into datacenter/enterprise ASICs 
and smart automotive solutions. The datacenter ASIC business is the k ey for upside, in our view, and 
we expect TPU to provide a volume boost for MTK, while the market will continue to monitor for its 
execution.

## Document 245: GFHK - UMC update

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - UMC update.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meaningful Re‑Rating Path into 2H26–2028

Maintained  Buy 
 Target price NT$215

Raise TP to NT$215: UMC’s share price underperformed Taiex in July/Aug but rebounded on 
rising expectations of price hikes and optical opportunities ( our report : PIC/TFLN Upside  
Unlocks a Higher -Margin Cycle  on Aug 24 ). We continue to expect UMC to be re ‑rated, 
supported by profitability optimization through 2H26–2027 (higher UTR, price hikes, improving 
mix, etc), followed by a shift to a new growth phase from 2028 driven by FinFET 12nm and an 
“optical full‑solution model”. Reflecting stronger pricing and margins, we lift 2026E/2027E EPS 
by +2%/+8% and raise our target multiple to 25x 2027E EPS on a stronger margin/growth 
outlook, resulting in a new TP of NT$215. Our model indicates UMC should maintain solid 
FCF and dividend yields. 
Earnings optimization in 2H26–2027 amid transfer‑order gains: We see UMC is in a phase 
of profitability optimization supported by rising UTR, pricing, and improving mix. We expect 
UTR for: 1) 12” to reach ~93% by end ‑2026 on strong DDIC volumes from Novatek and 
Samsung LSI at 22/28nm and Apple’s Mac  at 55/65nm, with upside from Sony’s sensors 
(>10KPM at 22/28nm); 2) 8” to ~90% by end ‑2026 and full loading by 2H27  on intact 
demand (datacenter PMIC, MCU, embedded memory, etc), Samsung LSI’s scale ‑down in 8” 
driving PMIC order transfer (~70KPM in 2027, ~110KPM in 2028), and additional transfer from 
Novatek (HV) and MTK (BCD), with upside from Qualcomm PMIC in 2027. Meanwhile, pricing 
momentum extends from 2H26 (selective customers) into 1H27 (10 –30% across 12” and 8”) 
and 2H27 (ongoing negotiations), suggesting visibility extending into 2H27.  
Transformation from 2028 to “asset‑light FinFET” and “optical full solution”: We expect 
UMC’s next growth phase, likely beginning 2028, to be driven by: 1) Intel 12nm as a major 
inflection—an asset‑light FinFET model with revenue contribution from 2028, plus upside from 
further 12nm expansion tied to Apple’s DDIC migration amid “Made in U.S.”; 2) SiPh/TFLN 
scaling toward 2.4T/3.2T, with TFLN as the largest long ‑term opportunity. We estimate EPS 
contribution of NT$0.7/NT$1.6 in 2027E/2028E, supported by higher ASPs of ~$4.5k and 
wafer volumes of 20KPM/40KPM by end‑2027/2028; 3) interposer and advanced packaging 
beginning to contribute from 2H27 (interposer), supported by emerging vectors such as silicon 
capacitor/DTC, hybrid bonding, and controller ‑to‑bonding integration, positioning UMC to 
evolve beyond PIC foundry toward a broader optical integration model. 
Risks: 1) Geo-political risk; 2) Demand uncertainties; 3) Competition; 4) New product delay.

Jeff Pu, CFA

Company Report | UMC 
4

Valuation and Recommendation  
Buy with TP of NT$215 
We rate UMC (2303 TT, UMC US) Buy and set our TP at NT$ 215, which is based on 25x our 2027E 
EPS estimate  from previous 20x due to enhanced margin and growth outlook. We adopt P/E 
methodology as we believe the market will be mainly focused on the company’s earnings outlook, and 
its earnings volatility has reduced since 2021 due to a much more diversified application mix and 
specialty position. We adopt the 2027 P/E valuation is due to the improving pricing environment in the 
longer term. Overall, we believe the  mature node industry outlook has become more favorable , and 
UMC’s mgmt. also guided a favorable pricing environment in 2026/2027. 
UMC P/E traded at an average of 1 5x since 2021, when the market accepted the P/E valuation.  Our 
target price is higher than the mid-cycle level, as we believe the rerating  catalysts are emerging amid 
favorable industry supply/demand. Meanwhile, the company continued to gain AI related business (i.e., 
networking, power, silicon photonics, advanced packaging) . We expect its ROE to reach mid -20s in 
2026E-2028E, vs. 11-12% in 2024-2025.

前 日 收 盤 價                              81 .3 0 元

許哲豪 
Jeff.syu@ctbcsis.com

GIS-KY(6456 TT)       法說 /座談摘要  
光通訊及 AR/VR 相關新事業有 望 2027 、2028 年帶來顯 著貢獻  
⚫ 營收將自 2Q26 起逐季轉佳  
⚫ 雷射封測試 量產進行中，預期年底 出貨， 2027 將有顯著 貢獻  
結論： GIS-KY 1H26 因終端零組件缺貨導致營收顯著下滑並陷入虧損，
但整體財務結構與淨現金部位依然穩健。展望 2H26，隨著記憶體短缺問
題逐步舒緩，營運預期將逐季回溫。公司目前正積極推動業務轉型，從
傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學領域，並已擴大資本支出佈局光通訊雷射
光源封測與 AR/VR 等新產品 ， 預計這些新事業將於2027 至 2028 年成為
公司顯著的營收成長動能 。

2Q26 財務數據  
⚫ 營收 105 億元(QoQ-34.7% ，YoY-44.8%)  
⚫ 毛利率 3.3%(QoQ -1.7ppts ，YoY-3.6ppts)  
⚫ 營業利益率 -7.6%(QoQ -5.6ppts ，YoY-8.7ppts)  
⚫ 稅後淨利 -10.53 億元(QoQ-415% ，YoY-2592%)  
⚫ 單季 EPS -3.15 元 
產品營收占比  
⚫ 2Q26：平板佔 42%、筆電佔 33%、指紋辨識模組佔 14%、其他佔
11%。 
⚫ 1H26：平板佔 44%、筆電佔 32%、指紋辨識模組佔 15%、其他佔
9%。 
法說會內容摘要  
1. 2Q26 營收下滑主因為公司目前主要出貨產品以平板與筆電為主，
終端客戶受到記憶體等零組件缺貨影響而無法順利拉貨，導致營收
顯著下降。而毛利下降是因為營收縮小，導致折舊等製造與固定費
用無法獲得足夠的營收進行攤銷。  
2. 因 2Q26 進行大陸子公司盈餘匯回母公司的資金安排，須繳納相關
所得稅費用，導致最終稅後淨損達 10.53 億元，淨利率為負 10%。 
3. 公司轉型大方向維持不變，致力於從傳統的觸控顯示模組使用者介
面 (User Interface) ，轉向半導體光學相關元件發展。  
4. 公司將持續佈局 Micro LED、Waveguide、AR HUD 以及 Optical Lens
等產品線，期盼未來能有具體成果展現。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

2

Q&A 內容摘要  
1.Q：3Q26 及 4Q26 的營運展望為何？  
A：7、8 月的單月營收約在 40 億元上下，較 2Q26 單月約 30 億元表現好，主要是記憶體缺貨
情況有稍微緩解。整體來看，3Q26 營收狀況會比 2Q26 好；而記憶體短缺的問題預計在 4Q26
會陸續得到舒緩，因此預估4Q26 營收會相對於 3Q26 更好，具體成長幅度待下次法說會詳細說
明。

2.Q：Sharp 龜山面板廠關廠對公司的影響為何？  
A：夏普是公司平板與筆電 TFT 面板的重要來源，其龜山廠在 8 月底已關廠，確實對 TFT 面板
來源有顯著影響。但終端市場正陸續轉向 OLED 面板，公司前幾年已預判此趨勢並推動轉型，
關廠只是讓此事提前發生 。 面板來源主要影響液晶顯示模組 (LCM) 後段業務 ， 但在Touch Panel
貼合業務上，即使轉用 OLED 仍可應用，受影響不大，同時公司也不排除尋找其他面板來源。

3.Q：最近 9 月初公告的資本支出用途為何？GIS 目前在光通訊領域的投資及產品是什麼？  
A：9 月初董事會核准台灣及越南子公司合計約 33 億元的資本支出，主要用於光通訊雷射光源
的封裝與測試設備，以因應終端龐大的需求。相關設備移入與實際進入量產的時間點，預估會
落在 1H27 之後。

4.Q：2026 年全年的資本支出大概為多少？項目為何？  
A：年初原本預估全年資本支出為 50 至 60 億元，但因客戶需求擴大，目前公告核准的資本支
出總額已達 80 多億元。項目包含光通訊雷射光源封測（約40 至 50 億元），以及新產品量產、
光學、AR 相關投資（約 10 至 20 億元）。這 80 億元不會集中在今年一次性現金流出，而是會
分散在今年與明年陸續發生。

5.Q：下半年是否同上半年一樣會有盈餘匯回母公司產生的稅務費用發生？  
A：公司 6 月初已公告大陸子公司有約 10 億人民幣盈餘要匯回。此金額有一大部分已於上半年
匯回，但另一部分會在下半年匯回，因此下半年的確還是會產生相關所得稅費用，不過金額不
會像上半年那麼龐大與顯著。

6.Q：越南廠目前的投資進度大概如何？ 
 A：越南廠土建即將完工，接下來將進行內部機電設備施工，預計今年底全部完工，年底到明
年初會有部分產品量產。越南廠主要生產大客戶的觸控模組，另有一部分光通訊雷射光源封裝
測試也會在該廠生產；因應物流運籌與策略考量，目前也正與其他客戶協調廠地規劃與產能。

### Page 3

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

3 
7.Q：公司發展的新產品（包含光波導、光通訊等）目前的發展狀況為何？ 
 A：光通訊雷射光源封測的第一階段機台裝機與試量產正在進行，順利的話預計今年底前開始
出貨，明年起會有較顯著營收貢獻。光波導與AR/VR 相關產品正與客戶密切開發中，考量客戶
計畫變化與產品組合要求，預計要到 2027 年甚至 2028 年才會有較顯著的貢獻。

8.Q：關於指紋辨識產品的展望為何？ 
 A：主要提供應用於手機與平板的超聲波與電容式指紋辨識模組。中短期仍受終端零組件（記
憶體）缺貨影響出貨動能，後續若要有顯著營收成長，須期待終端缺貨狀況獲得進一步緩解。

9.Q：請問對明年展望怎麼看？新事業明年的營收貢獻預估會到哪裡？  
A：期望光通訊及 AR/VR 相關新事業在 2027、2028 年能帶來顯著貢獻。但明年具體貢獻程度
因終端市場與客戶動態變化大，可能要等到下一季季底才會比較明朗；但公司整體轉型的發展
方向不會有太大改變。

10.Q：今年在雷射光源預計的資本支出大約是 40 到 50 億元，9 月份公告的是 33 億元，是否代
表先前已有 10 到 20 億元已經投入並且即將投產？  
A：是的，去年底或今年初已陸續投入約 10 多億元於雷射光源封裝，設備已進駐並進行小量測
試與量產爬坡階段，預計 4Q26 就能產生相對的貢獻。9 月公告的 33 億元是後續再增加的擴產
資本支出。

11.Q：雷射光源封裝產能擴充後，預估 2027 年的月產能大概可以達到多少 KK？  
A：因終端市場需求非常強勁，目前公司重點在調校製程良率與生產效率，故不方便提供明確
的月產能數字，待年底將良率與效率調整到位後，再向外界做較完整的報告。

12.Q：雷射光源目前總共有幾個客戶或潛在客戶？在光通訊領域除了雷射光源外是否還有其他
機會？  
A：目前接洽對象以國際大廠為主，有些訂單已確定，有些在洽談中，不便透露具體數量或客
戶名單。期望藉由此次業務在光通訊領域站穩腳步，內部有研發多種新產品，待取得客戶信任
與認證後，會往產業鏈上下游其他相關業務發展。

13.Q：如何看待雷射光源封裝的毛利率？ 
 A：接單前內部都會評估過毛利率，達標才會接單並通過投資案。理論值雖然可以掌握，但實
際毛利率需待進入一定規模量產，產生實際營收與成本費用後才會明確；公司策略上也會優先
承接毛利率較高的高附加價值訂單。

### Page 4

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

4 
14.Q：雷射光源封裝的 Know-how 與既有業務重疊性高嗎？主要進入門檻為設備還是人員經
驗？  
A：公司過去在電容式指紋辨識等領域已累積了長期的IC 測試與封裝經驗，並獲國際大廠認證
出貨。因此無論是人員、設備、經驗或技術 Know-how 皆具備一定實力，跨入該領域並非單一
因素門檻，而是多種經驗綜合累積的結果。

15.Q：雷射封裝測試上游原料是自行取得還是客戶指定？下游客戶是光模組廠還是CSP 廠？有
無針對特定傳輸規模（如 400G 或 800G）著墨？  
A：上游原料視不同客戶情況而定；下游客戶目前尚未直接接觸到CSP 廠，客戶商業樣態各有
不同。未來目標會以 800G 以上、較高功率與高階的晶片封裝服務來進行佈局。

16.Q：光通訊後續還有機會增加資本投入嗎？以目前 40 到 50 億的資本支出來說，兩三年回本
的機率高嗎？  
A： 期望藉由此次投資在光通訊產業鏈建立客戶信任 ，若未來評估有可行的新領域會持續跨足，
屆時就可能有增加資本投入的需求。公司當然希望能高機率回本，因此重點是提升生產良率與
效率，並穩固客戶關係，達成後回本的機率就會相對較高。

17.Q：雷射技術是以 EML 還是 CW 為主？  
A：這取決於客戶的需求，目前不管 EML 或 CW，公司的技術與能力都可以做到。

Style Exposure
A new wave of cold-plate upgrades is setting up a multi-year content step-up in AI

server liquid cooling, and we see A VC as the clearest winner. We assume coverage at
Overweight with a Street-high December 2027 price target of NT$5,200, based on a
22x 2028E P/E, which implies ~55% upside from the current share price.
Our 2026/27/28 estimares are 3%/16%/16% higher than Bloomberg consensus,
driven by the four tangible earnings levers we expect: (1) ASP increases on select
switch-tray cold plates that lift 2H26 revenue and gross margin via a better mix/yield;

(2) double-sided cold-plate designs plus diamond and copper adoption that raises
content in Nvidia VR Ultra (VRU) systems from 2Q/2H27; (3) broader ASIC cooling
complexity into 2H27/2028 (Google TPU v9, AWS Trn4, etc.), and (4) rising liquid
cooling adoption in OSFP (Octal Small Form-factor Pluggable)/ELSFP (External
Laser Small Form-Factor Pluggable) optical modules. We expect A VC’s share price
to continue outperforming with faster earnings upgrades over the next 6-12

months. With this note, we transfer coverage of Asia Vital Components to Megan
Hsueh.
• Double-sided cold plate design potentially for Nvidia VRU and Google
TPU v9, in addition to AWS Trn3 and AMD MI450; diamond copper to
add value. We believe the double-sided cold plate design is already set to be
adopted in Trn3 and MI450 compute trays from 4Q26 onwards and will likely
be adopted in VRU compute trays as well from 2Q/2H27 onwards, suggesting
incremental cold plates for backside cooling. In addition, we believe VRU cold

plates may start adopting the diamond and copper material. Both the additional
cold plate units and the new material adoption would increase the cooling
content meaningfully for VRU compute trays (US$3k-3.5k per tray, 50%
higher vs the previous generation). TPU v8 also creates decent cooling content,
due to its gold-plating design for TPU cold plates as well as additional cold
plate units for surrounding ICs. As for TPU v9, we believe a larger die size and

higher TDP will increase the likelihood of double-sided cold plate adoption. In
our view, A VC has secured major shares in these GPU and ASIC projects with
meaningful spec upgrades, making it the main beneficiary of higher content
growth than market expectations.
• More GM upside on enhanced mix and yield, along with a successful ASP
increase for certain cold plate projects. We expect A VC to deliver further
GM upside into 3Q and 4Q26 by reaching the 34% and 35% level (vs consensus

at ~33% and 34%), suggesting solid growth vs 1Q/2Q GM of 30% and 33%,
driven by a better product mix (more ASIC contribution) and increasing yield/
automation. Also, we believe A VC has successfully raised ASP for some of its
cold plate products (mainly for Nvidia/Mellanox switch trays, in our view) to
reflect higher costs from materials and labor overtime pay, helping to maintain
its margin while benefiting its revenue growth momentum. Coupled with the

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
We are OW on A VC for its strong earnings growth potential,
which we think is supported by the content increase in AI
servers. We expect A VC to see increasing upside from more
liquid cooling opportunities, with much higher ASP and margin,
while its competitive advantages vs. peers could give it further
market share gains as the industry’s structural trends develop.

Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$5,200 is based on 22x 2028E P/E.   The
target multiple is at the mid to high end of A VC’s historical
average trading range of 15-25x P/E since the start of the AI
server boom, as we expect an earnings upgrade cycle (based on
our above-consensus estimates in 2027-28) to support this
valuation.

Key risks to ourestimates, price target and rating
• Slower-than-expected spec upgrades in future AI systems: We believe AI GPU
and ASIC customers’ new product generation launches will be the main driver of
continued cooling spec upgrades. However, if the launch timing of next-gen
products is delayed or spec improvement is limited, A VC’s growth estimates may
have to be slightly moderated.
• Market share loss: We regard share loss as a milder risk, as the entry barrier for

cold plate modules has become higher, especially because the AI server migration
cadence remains at the short 1-2 year cycle. Coupled with customers’ preference for
a limited numbers of suppliers (usually 2-3), we do not think there will be a major
market share shift.
• ASP/margin pressure: As Nvidia has put cold plates into its “Group A”
components, meaning standardization, from the VR200 generation, investors may be
worried about the potential ASP and GM pressure, given less differentiation among

peers. However, we believe A VC’s 2Q26 and potentially 2H26 GM may continue to
beat investor expectations with stronger expansion, thanks to its continued yield
enhancement, better-than-expected cooling content growth with its stable market
share and rising ASIC contribution (with higher content and margin vs GPU
projects).

Performance and Valuation
Share price performance
Figure 1: AVC’s share price vs the Taiex
Source: Bloomberg Finance L.P.
• In 2025, A VC’s share price underperformed in 1H given market concerns about cold
plate ASP/margin decline when Nvidia added cold plates to its Group A components
for VR200 (implying standardization). However, the share price outperformed in 2H

given the confirmation of a total content value increase of “tray level” thanks to the
fanless design of VR200 with an additional number of cold plates being adopted per
tray.
• In 1H26, A VC’s share price continued to outperform the Taiex with new TAM
expansion opportunities from both ASICs and Nvidia’s new rack solutions, such as
LPX rack, Vera CPU rack and CPO-related solutions.
• In 3Q26, A VC’s share price corrected meaningfully (even more than other AI sub-

sectors) in July, given the overall market pullback from de-leveraging and
incrementally from the delay of VR200. However, it has rebounded rapidly since
August thanks to 1) a reported GM/EPS numbers beat, 2) a smoother VR200 ramp
in late 3Q26, and 3) the higher-content ASIC projects being expected to enter mass
production in 4Q26 to drive further revenue and margin expansion.

Financial Analysis
We forecast A VC’s revenue to increase 61%/57%/22% YoY in 2026/27/28, supported by
the stronger-than-expected cold plate spec upgrades and content growth in both GPU
and ASIC systems, with the company’s continued leading share in these high-spec
projects.
A VC’s 2Q26 gross margin expanded to 33% thanks to continued yield enhancement of

liquid cooling products. We believe the company’s margin will continue to surprise ro
the upside in 2H26-2028, driven by the near-term favorable product mix from VR200
and multiple new ASIC project ramp, along with longer-term continued spec upgrades,
which support our gross margin forecast of 33%/36%/37% in 2026/27/28. The stronger
GM growth also serves as the key driver of our bottom-line EPS growth forecasts of
120%/73%/27% in 2026/27/28.

J P M O R G A N
Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Asia Vital Components (Overweight; Price Target: NT$5,200.00)
Investment Thesis
We are OW on A VC for its strong earnings growth potential, which we think is supported
by the content increase in AI servers. We expect A VC to see increasing upside from more
liquid cooling opportunities, with much higher ASP and margin, while its competitive

advantages vs. peers could give it further market share gains as the industry’s structural
trends develop.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$5,200 is based on 22x 2028E P/E.  The target multiple is at the mid
to high end of A VC’s historical average trading range of 15-25x P/E since the start of the
AI server boom, as we expect an earnings upgrade cycle (based on our above-consensus
estimates in 2027-28) to support this valuation.
Risks to Rating and Price Target

Key downside risks are: (1) slower-than-expected spec upgrades to future AI systems; (2)
market share losses; and (3) ASP/margin pressure from the competition.
This document is being provided for the exclusive use of Tommy Liou at Cathay Life Insurance Co., Ltd..

## Document 262: JPM_Taiwan_Cooling_Compo_2026-09-29_5458761 NV VRU AWS GOOGLE

Source PDF: all report pdf/JPM_Taiwan_Cooling_Compo_2026-09-29_5458761 NV VRU AWS GOOGLE.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

J.P. Morgan Securities (Asia Pacific) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Broking (Hong Kong) Limited
Albert Hung
(886-2) 2725-9875
albert.hung@jpmchase.com
J.P. Morgan Securities (Taiwan) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Securities (Asia Pacific) Limited/ J.P.
Morgan Broking (Hong Kong) Limited
We see an accelerating cooling spec upgrade trend in both AI ASICs and GPU
systems, and the content uplift looks larger than investors’ expectations, especially
for Nvidia’s next-gen VR Ultra (VRU), contrary to recent market concerns on

potential de-specing. We continue to believe that cooling is the key bottleneck to
unlocking continued TDP growth of all AI chips and migration of AI server
systems. For VRU, we see new designs and spec upgrades across cold plate
(double-sided design and diamond copper material adoption) , quick
disconnect (MQD upgrading to ZQD),  and lid/heat spreader (3-piece/
removable lid design). We believe the stronger-than-expected content growth in
Nvidia VRU, along with the further increasing ASIC and AMD GPU cooling value

will continue to benefit the leading suppliers of each component. Looking forward
to the next 1-2 years, we believe the CPO/NPO switches and higher-spec optical
transceivers/modules will become the next driver with mainstream adoption of
liquid cooling in future generations (likely from 1.6T/3.2T gens). We prefer A VC
(OW) and Fositek (OW) in the cooling space.
• Cold plate: double-sided design likely adopted in Nvidia VRU, AWS Trn3
and AMD MI450 to drive +50% content; diamond copper to add value.

The double-sided cold plate design looks set to become the next step-up in
cooling specs. We expect adoption in AWS Trainium 3 (Trn3) and AMD
MI450 compute trays, with Nvidia VRU likely next. This new double-sided
design was required given the VPD (Vertical Power Delivery) architecture,
which moves the power discretes, SOCAMM, and capacitors to the backside
of chip board, requiring incremental cold plates for backside cooling. VRU
Bianca cold plates may also upgrade their material to “diamond copper” for

better cooling effectiveness, which adds further value per unit. This
combination supports meaningful cooling content upside for VRU (~50%
increase vs. VR200) versus market expectations for flat-to-down cooling
content on de-spec concerns. Outside these platforms, Google TPU v8 should
drive solid cooling content via gold-plated cold plates plus additional units
adopted for surrounding ICs. TPU v9’s larger chip die and higher TDP also
increase the odds of the double-sided cold plate adoption.

• Quick disconnect (QD): No content change with the potential VRU 9-
switch tray design of 1.5U with “double-layer architecture”; meaningful
upgrades in VRU (ZQD adoption) with ~100% content increase per rack.
In the VRU system, we believe Nvidia will upgrade some of its QDs from the
previous MQD (Multi Quick Disconnect)/UQD (Universal Quick Disconnect)
to the higher-spec ZQD (Zero-drip/flow rate Quick Disconnect) with ~2x ASP,
in order to help improve the cooling efficiency with better liquid flow rate and

limited leakage. Per JPMe, the total QD content per VRU NVL72 rack will
increase by ~100% vs VR200/GB300 racks, as the ZQD with 2x ASP will be
adopted in 1) the compute tray Bianca modules (upgrading to ZQD06 spec
along with GPU cold plate upgrades), 2) entry QDs for all 9 compute trays and
9 “double-layered” switch trays (the 2 out-of-tray plug QDs upgrading to
ZQD14 spec), and 3) rack manifold socket QDs (upgrading to ZQD14 as well).
• Heat spreader: No delay of 3-piece/removable lid adoption in Rubin Ultra,

Investment thesis
We believe the cooling component sector will continue to outperform the market, and in
this space, we prefer A VC and Fositek. Compared to other cooling names, these two
names 1) still trade at an undemanding valuation (vs historical) with near-term share
price performance underperforming other cooling names (or even Taiex for A VC), 2)

can continue to deliver both near-term and longer-term earnings upside (e.g. 3Q margin
beat, 2028/28E EPS upside from more meaningful spec upgrades), and 3) serve as the
leading players in the specific components, enabling the major beneficiaries of spec
upgrades and ongoing margin expansion (with smaller-than-expected ASP pressure).

Compute tray level: cooling content to increase another 30-50% in VRU driven by
double-sided cold plate adding diamond copper materials and ZQD adoption

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of VRU compute tray can reach US$3,000-3,500 level, which suggests
30-50% content increase vs current VR design, and should be better than market
expectation of limited changes or even de-spec. The stronger content growth in
VRU compute tray is mainly driven by 1) the double-sided cold plate design for
Bianca modules given VPD (vertical power delivery) adoption (placed at the

backside of chip): making it 6 cold plates (2 existing + 4 at the backside for rack
GPU) on the upper half of tray for the two Bianca modules (vs 2 CP units in VR
upper-half tray) + the existing 3 cold plates at the bottom half for the CX-9 chips
and power distribution board (PDB), 2) diamond copper material adoption:
specifically added for the cold plate areas attaching on HBMs, 3) additional QDs
required (additional 4 sets/8 units of Mini QD, in our view) for the additional cold

plates (the ones for the VPD placed at the backside of chip), and 4) QDs for the
Bianca module cold plates upgrading to ZQD from MQD previously with ~2x
ASP increase.
• QD: In VRU system, we believe Nvidia will upgrade some of its QDs from the
previous MQD (Multi Quick Disconnect)/UQD (Universal Quick Disconnect) to the
higher-spec ZQD (Zero-drip/flow rate Quick Disconnect) to help improve the
cooling efficiency with better liquid flow rate and limited leakage. Hence, we

VR, implying an increase to (8+2) units from (4+2) units, given 2 layers/boards per
tray. Although the 8 units of QD inside the VRU switch tray should all remain with

the MQD06 spec, the 2x unit increase still drives overall QD content growth. In
addition, the 2 QDs outside of VRU switch tray will be upgraded to ZQD14 from
UQD08 in VR, which also adds incremental content from ASP increase for these 2
plug QDs. In total, we model the QD content per switch tray to increase to ~US$260
for VRU vs ~US$150 for VR, based on our estimates.

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of AWS Trn3 compute tray can reach US$3,000-3,500 level (~30% higher
than Nvidia’s VR compute tray), thanks mainly to the double-sided cold plate
design (8 CPs for 4 ASICs) plus 1 incremental tray-sized cold plate for the

baseboard with decent amount of QD adoption. Also, the Trn3 switch tray may also
create US$1,500-2,000 content value, with slightly more trays per rack (10 switch
trays in Teton 3 Max vs 9 units in VR NVL72 rack). Therefore, the total cooling
content (including cold plate, QD and inner manifold) per Teton 3 Max rack
could reach ~US$86k (vs <US$70k content for VR NVL72 rack).
• QD: AWS Teton 3 Max adopts UQD and MQD in their Trn3 compute and switch

tray designs, and the total QD unit adoption per Teton 3 Max rack is 512 units vs
450 units in Nvidia VR NVL72 rack (both excluding rack manifold socket QDs).
Each QD ASP is at ~US$20, so the total QD content per AWS Teton 3 Max (Trn3
liquid-cooled rack) is ~US$10.2k per NRL72 rack.

• Cold plate: We believe the cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner
manifold) of TPU v8 compute tray can reach US$2-2.5k level, similar to Nvidia’s
VR compute tray despite lower chip TDP, due to the more complicated cold plate
design, including 1) more cold plate unit adoption: a total of 12 cold plates with 4

big ones for TPUs and an additional 8 small ones for surrounding chips, and 2) gold-
plating design (given metal TIM adoption) to add incremental content. Therefore,
the total cooling content (including cold plate, QD and inner manifold) per TPU
v8 rack could reach ~US$38k.
• QD: Google TPUv8 compute tray adopts NSP1 QD, unlike Nvidia or other CSP
ASIC systems that generally use MQD or UQD, and it only places 2 units of NSP1
QD per TPU compute tray (vs 22 units in VR and Trn3 compute tray). In terms of

ASP, we believe NSP1 QD is 2-3x higher than both MQD and UQD. However,
based on our calculation, the total QD content of Google TPU is relatively small
at ~US$1.8k with only 32 units per rack (vs 400 or 500 unit level for VR and Trn3
rack), despite higher ASP.
• Heat spreader: Google TPUs also adopt stiffeners for their heat spreader design,
including both v8i and v8t. However, for TPU v9, we believe the removable lid +
stiffener could be a potential solution, given much bigger size and higher TDP

(potentially reaching 2,000W), which may drive ~10x content increase if the new
design is actually adopted. This is yet to be confirmed, but we believe the spec
ugprade trend is certain.

the duration of the price target indicated in this report, we expect this stock will perform in line with the average total return of the stocks in the
Research Analyst’s, or the Research Analyst’s team’s, coverage universe); and Underweight (over the duration of the price target indicated in

this report, we expect this stock will underperform the average total return of the stocks in the Research Analyst’s, or the Research Analyst’s
team’s, coverage universe. NR is Not Rated. In this case, J.P. Morgan has removed the rating and, if applicable, the price target, for this stock
because of either a lack of a sufficient fundamental basis or for legal, regulatory or policy reasons. The previous rating and, if applicable, the
price target, no longer should be relied upon. An NR designation is not a recommendation or a rating. Some stocks under coverage have a rating

but no price target; in these cases, we expect the stock will outperform/perform in line/underperform the average total return of the stocks in the
Research Analyst’s, or the Research Analyst’s team’s, coverage universe of the relevant duration of the region. In our Asia (ex-Australia and ex-

### Page 1

M
Update
Makalot Industrial  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Sales In-Line; Attractive
Entry Point; Keep OW
September sales reached NT$2.93bn (+24% YoY/−18% MoM), driven by shipment
growth of 22% YoY, ASP down 1%, and the balance from FX. Management noted
that shipments worth NT$80mn were delayed to next month due to volatile
shipping schedules.
3Q26 sales (+7% YoY) were broadly in line with both our estimates and consensus.
Shipments rose 1% (slightly below expectations due to shipment delays and order

cherry-picking), ASP was flat, and FX contributed +6%. Based on our simulations,
July and August GPM should exceed 3Q26 guidance of 24%.
Makalot kept 4Q26 guidance unchanged, with shipments expected to grow at a low-
single-digit YoY rate, ASP up 5–7% (supported by cherry-picking higher-ASP, higher-
profit orders), and GPM at 26% (-0.7ppt YoY).
We continue to expect Makalot's 2026 earnings to return close to peak levels. Our
net income forecast of NT$3.9bn is back to the 2023 level and only a low-single-

digit percentage below the record achieved in 2024. We believe macro uncertainties
are already priced in and view the current level as an attractive entry point, with
valuation at 11.3x our 2H26–1H27 EPS estimate and a 7.9% cash yield based on our
projected 2026 dividend.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Terence Cheng

### Page 2

M
Update
2
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Makalot Industrial (1477.TW)
Base case value, derived by applying a multiple of 17x to our 2026 EPS estimate. Our target
multiple is below its average P/E ratio in the prior up-cycle of 21x (after re-rating in 2H23), and
is within the stock's 10-year historical 9-29x P/E range. We view this as reasonable, with 2026e
earnings growth of 8% seen outperforming apparel and footwear peers with flat to single-
digit growth.

Risks to Upside
n New non-US customer wins
n Faster-than-expected inventory digestion
n ASP and margin expansion via more complex garments
n Better US GDP growth
n Faster order reallocation owing to trade tension
Risks to Downside
n Deterioration in the macro environment
n Drastic appreciation of TWD
n Pricing competition initiated from competitors
n Tariff increases on apparel exports from Vietnam or Cambodia

## Document 314: The Bond Yield Spectrum

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Bond Yield Spectrum.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: The Bond Yield Spectrum
Date:    5 hours ago
The Bond Yield Spectrum

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
The Bond Yield Spectrum
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 7 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Adam Kring / Unsplash

I. From The Good To The Bad To The Ugly
The spectrum of explanations for the rise in bond yields worldwide
includes relatively benign ones to downright dangerous ones (chart). On
the good side of the spectrum, better-than-expected economic growth has
driven US bond yields up to levels that are back to normal. On the ugly
side, soaring bond yields in France are signaling a looming debt crisis. In
between are bad scenarios that explain the synchronized rise in global

### Page 2

bond yields. These include the inflationary energy shock attributable to the
war in the Middle East and the unwinding of the yen-carry trade.
Let's have a closer look at the good, the bad, and the ugly:
(1) R-star rising. R-star, or the neutral interest rate, is the real interest
rate consistent with low unemployment and price stability. It balances the
supply of savings with demand for investment. Three forces are pushing it
higher.
First, demand for capital is booming along with AI-related capital

spending. Hyperscaler capex is expected to reach $750-$800 billion this
year and $1.2 trillion next. Total US corporate bond issuance over the past
12 months through August was a record $3.0 trillion, including $1.4 trillion
and $1.6 trillion issued by nonfinancial and financial corporations,
respectively (chart).

### Page 3

At the same time, US Treasury borrowing totaled $2.1 trillion over the past
12 months through September, including $1.3 trillion in notes and bonds
(chart).
Second, national savings faces demographic headwinds as retiring Baby
Boomers stop saving and draw down their net worth (chart).

### Page 4

Third, stronger productivity growth may be raising the return on capital
and the economy’s sustainable growth rate (chart).
The Fed's 175bps cuts in the federal funds rate since September 2024
have been completely offset by a comparable increase in the 10-year
Treasury bond yield since then. Even at the start of this year, most Fed
officials believed that the FFR was modestly restrictive, i.e., that it
exceeded the neutral rate. In September, they acknowledged their error by

hiking the FFR by 25 basis points, as Fed Chair Kevin Warsh said they
had removed a "dose of accommodation," implying that the FFR is
actually below the neutral rate!

### Page 5

(2) Energy shock. Moving toward the middle of the spectrum are higher-
for-longer oil prices. With US-Iran talks stalled and the US increasing its
military assets, the risk of renewed escalation remains high. The longer
energy costs stay elevated, the greater the risk they'll boost core inflation,
forcing a more aggressive Fed response. If crude oil prices fall because
more oil is getting through the Strait of Hormuz, diesel prices might remain
elevated (chart).

(3) Unwinding yen-carry trade. The yen-carry trade was based on ultra-
low Japanese interest rates and a stable or weak yen. Now the BOJ is
raising interest rates and the yen may be bottoming (charts). This is
forcing traders to liquidate bond holdings previously financed with cheap
Japanese money.

### Page 6

(4) Debt crisis. At the far end of the spectrum is the "Revenge of the
Bond Vigilantes." Between the Great Financial Crisis and the Great Virus
Crisis, major central banks' ultra-easy policies enabled governments to
run large deficits. The Bond Vigilantes were powerless. But now they are
more powerful than ever because government debt is at record highs.
Central banks have been forced to raise their policy rates as inflation has
been more troublesome in recent years. Bond yields have soared this

year worldwide as inflation rebounded and fiscal-risk premiums have
risen.
The danger is a self-reinforcing debt spiral: Higher yields raise interest
costs, widen deficits, require more borrowing, and push yields higher still
(chart). And, of course, higher yields increase the risk of causing a
recession, which would exacerbate any debt crisis.

### Page 7

In our view, the US should remain in the higher R-star end of the
spectrum. We would start to worry about the debt-crisis danger zone if the
US bond yield rises above nominal GDP growth (chart).
In France, on the other hand, the Bond Vigilantes are pushing the 10-year
government bond yield well above nominal GDP growth (chart). The
French could be toast if yields continue to soar.
II. The Fed Minutes
The latest Fed minutes paint a familiar picture: a resilient economy, a firm

labor market, and inflation that remains too high. We drew three key
takeaways from the minutes of the Fed’s September 15-16 meeting.

### Page 8

(1) Restrictiveness. Several participants said the current policy rate was
not restrictive or only mildly restrictive, while a couple explicitly raised their
estimates of the neutral federal funds rate. That fits neatly with our R-star
story.
(2) Macro backdrop. The Fed saw a resilient economy: real GDP is
expanding at a solid pace, consumer spending has firmed, AI investment
remains robust, and the labor market is close to maximum employment. At

the same time, inflation remains elevated and risks were still skewed to
the upside.
(3) Rate outlook. All participants backed September’s 25bps hike, and
most judged that another increase would likely be appropriate by year-
end.
💡
